Model: mistral-small-3.1-24b

--- Model Response ---
To find the shortest path between the two black nodes \( S \) and \( Q \), we need to analyze the connections and determine the sequence of nodes that results in the shortest path.

1. **Start at node \( S \):**
   - \( S \) is connected to nodes \( E \) and \( F \).

2. **From \( E \):**
   - \( E \) is connected to nodes \( A \) and \( B \).

3. **From \( F \):**
   - \( F \) is connected to nodes \( B \) and \( T \).

4. **From \( B \):**
   - \( B \) is connected to nodes \( C \), \( G \), \( E \), and \( N \).
   - \( C \) is connected to \( O \) and \( N \).
   - \( G \) is connected to nodes \( H \), \( I \), and \( T \).

5. **From \( T \):**
   - \( T \) is connected to nodes \( F \), \( G \), and \( J \).

6. **From \( G \):**
   - \( G \) is connected to \( B \), \( T \), \( H \), and \( I \).

7. **From \( H \):**
   - \( H \) is connected to nodes \( I \).

8. **From \( I \):**
   - \( I \) is connected to nodes \( H \), \( P \), \( R \), and \( Q \).

9. **From \( R \):**
   - \( R \) is connected to \( Q \).

From node \( S \) via \( F \) to \( T \) to \( G \) to \( I \) to \( Q \) is the path.
  Alternatively, consider node \( S \) via \( E \) and some intermediate which is less efficient and longer than previous path steps.

Thus, the shortest path from \( S \) to \( Q \) is through the following nodes:
\[ S \rightarrow F \rightarrow T \rightarrow G \rightarrow I \rightarrow Q \]

Length: 4, Path: [S, F, T, G, I, Q]
--- Ground Truth ---
{'length': 5, 'path': [['S', 'F', 'B', 'H', 'I', 'Q'], ['S', 'F', 'G', 'H', 'I', 'Q']]}

Model: mistral-small-3.1-24b

--- Model Response ---
To solve this problem, we need to identify the paths between the two black nodes, G and N, and find the minimum set of nodes whose removal will disconnect these paths.

1. **Identify the two black nodes:**
   - The black nodes are G and N.

2. **Find the immediate neighboring nodes of G and N:**
   - G is connected to: B, J, C
   - N is connected to: C, R

3. **Identify all path connections:**
   - From G:
     - G -> B -> E
     - G -> B -> S
     - G -> B -> C -> N
     - G -> B -> A -> S
     - G -> B -> A -> E
     - G -> B -> T
     - G -> B -> F
     - G -> J -> I -> H
     - G -> J -> I -> Q -> P
     - G -> J -> O -> R
     - G -> C -> N
     - G -> C -> S
   
   - From N:
     - N -> C -> B -> E
     - N -> C -> G
     - N -> C -> S
     - N -> C -> B -> A -> E
     - N -> C -> B -> A -> S
     - N -> C -> B -> T
     - N -> C -> B -> F
     - N -> C -> B -> J -> I -> H
     - N -> C -> B -> J -> I -> Q -> P
     - N -> C -> B -> J -> O -> R
     - N -> R

4. **Find the paths between G and N:**
   - Path 1: G -> C -> N
   - Path 2: G -> J -> O -> R -> N
   - Alternative paths can be merge from shared nodes, e.g. via B.

5. **Identify the bottleneck nodes:**
   - Removing C will disconnect G from N via path 1.
   - Removing O will disconnect G from N via path 2.

   However, we must ensure to checkout neighbors to see if removal involves necessary nodes.
   - Removing C disconnects all paths going via C.
   - O can be ruled out for minimum removal as C covers broaderpaths.

   Thus, conclusion C removal disruptions broadest paths.

**Output Format:**

Nodes to remove: 1, Cut Set: [C]
--- Ground Truth ---
{'length': 2, 'cut_set': ['B', 'C']}

--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes (S and O), we need to analyze the paths between these nodes.

1. **Identify the black nodes:** S and O.
2. **Find immediate neighboring nodes:**
   - Node S is connected to nodes: A, B, E.
   - Node O is connected to nodes: C, G, Q.

3. **Iteratively find other neighboring nodes:**
   - From S:
     - A connects to: E, F.
     - B connects to: E, T, G.
     - E connects to: A, F, T, B.
     - F connects to: A, E, T.
     - T connects to: B, E, F.
     - G (from B) connects to: B, O, J.
     - J (from G) connects to: G, H, I.
     - H (from J) connects to: J, I.
     - I (from J) connects to: J, H, P.
     - P (from I) connects to: Q.
   - From O:
     - C connects to: N.
     - G (already mentioned) connects to: B, O, J.
     - Q connects to: R, P.

4. **Evaluate the found paths:**
   - Paths from S to O:
     - S -> B -> G -> O
     - S -> B -> G -> J -> I -> P -> Q -> O

   To disconnect S and O, we need to remove edges that are common in all paths between S and O. The critical edges are:
   - (B, G)
   - (G, O)
   - (G, J) (though it's not necessary if we remove the direct connection G to O)

   Removing just (B, G) and (G, O) will disconnect S and O completely.

**Output Format:**

Edges to remove: 2, Cut Set: [(B, G), (G, O)].
